    Important Words
    • asūya — envy
      A-sūya — literally 'not bearing well' the success of another. The first of the six disqualifications. Caitanya Mahāprabhu's prayer in the Śikṣāṣṭakam (tṛṇād api sunīcena) is the direct antidote: humility removes the soil in which asūyā grows.
    • anṛta — untruthfulness
      A-ṛta — opposed to ṛta (cosmic order, truth-as-reality). Anṛta is not merely lying but living crosswise to the order. Prabhupāda glossed: a brāhmaṇa is one whose speech is anchored in Veda, hence in ṛta. Mantras spoken by anṛta-mouths cannot bear fruit.
    • dambha — false pride, ostentation
      From dabh, to deceive. Dambha is religiosity performed for show. The Gītā (16.4) lists it as the first āsurī-sampad. A brāhmaṇa free of dambha performs the saṁskāra without theatrical excess — Yaśodā's vipras chant; they do not perform.
    • satya-śīlānām — of those endowed with brahminical qualifications
      Satya (truth) + śīla (character). The compound names the inner disposition behind the six negations. Gauḍīya tradition reads brāhmaṇa as a qualification, not a birth-category — a satya-śīla Vaiṣṇava of any background carries the same potency.
    • abhiṣicya — after bathing (the child)
      Same root as abhiṣecanam (verse 4) — abhi-ṣic, to anoint. The bathing is a coronation in miniature, repeated each time the household needs to re-install Kṛṣṇa as the center after a disturbance.
    • mahā-guṇam — of excellent quality
      Mahā (great) + guṇa (quality). The food given to brāhmaṇas is first-class — Vaiṣṇava hospitality refuses to give leftovers or seconds to the Lord's servants. The principle: what you give to the brāhmaṇa is what reaches the Lord through him.
    When brāhmaṇas are free from envy, untruthfulness, unnecessary pride, grudges, disturbance by the opulence of others, and false prestige, their blessings never go in vain. Considering this, Nanda Mahārāja soberly took Kṛṣṇa on his lap and invited such truthful brāhmaṇas to perform a ritualistic ceremony according to the holy hymns of the Sāma Veda, Ṛg Veda and Yajur Veda. Then, while the hymns were being chanted, he bathed the child with water mixed with pure herbs, and after performing a fire ceremony, he sumptuously fed all the brāhmaṇas with first-class grains and other food.
    Purport

    Nanda Mahārāja was very confident about the qualifications of the brāhmaṇas and their blessings. He was fully confident that simply if the good brāhmaṇas showered their blessings, the child Kṛṣṇa would be happy. The blessings of qualified brāhmaṇas can bring happiness not only to Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, but to everyone. Because Kṛṣṇa is self-sufficient, He does not require anyone’s blessings, yet Nanda Mahārāja thought that Kṛṣṇa required the blessings of the brāhmaṇas. What then is to be said of others? In human society, therefore, there must be an ideal class of men, brāhmaṇas, who can bestow blessings upon others, namely, upon the kṣatriyas, vaiśyas and śūdras, so that everyone will be happy. Kṛṣṇa therefore says in Bhagavad-gītā (4.13) that human society must have four social orders ( cātur-varṇyaṁ mayā sṛṣṭaṁ guṇa-karma-vibhāgaśaḥ ); it is not that everyone should become a śūdra or a vaiśya and human society will prosper. As enunciated in Bhagavad-gītā, there must be a class of brāhmaṇas with qualities like satya (truthfulness), śama (peacefulness), dama (self-control) and titikṣā (tolerance). Here also, in the Bhāgavatam, Nanda Mahārāja invites qualified brāhmaṇas. There may be caste brāhmaṇas, and we have all respect for them, but their birth in brāhmaṇa families does not mean that they are qualified to bestow blessings upon the other members of human society. This is the verdict of the śāstras. In Kali-yuga, caste brāhmaṇas are accepted as brāhmaṇas. Vipratve sūtram eva hi ( Bhāg. 12.2.3 ): in Kali-yuga, simply by putting on a thread worth two paise, one becomes a brāhmaṇa. Such brāhmaṇas were not called for by Nanda Mahārāja. As stated by Nārada Muni ( Bhāg. 7.11.35 ), yasya yal lakṣaṇaṁ proktam. The symptoms of a brāhmaṇa are stated in śāstra, and one must be qualified with these symptoms. The blessings of brāhmaṇas who are not envious, disturbed or puffed up with pride and false prestige and who are fully qualified with truthfulness will be useful. Therefore a class of men must be trained as brāhmaṇas from the very beginning. Brahmacārī gurukule vasan dānto guror hitam ( Bhāg. 7.12.1 ). The word dāntaḥ is very important. Dāntaḥ refers to one who is not envious, disturbing or puffed up with false prestige. With the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, we are trying to introduce such brāhmaṇas in society. Brāhmaṇas must ultimately be Vaiṣṇavas, and if one is a Vaiṣṇava, he has already acquired the qualifications of a brāhmaṇa. Brahma-bhūtaḥ prasannātmā ( Bg. 18.54 ). The word brahma-bhūta refers to becoming a brāhmaṇa, or understanding what is Brahman ( brahma jānātīti brāhmaṇaḥ ). One who is brahma-bhūta is always happy ( prasannātmā ). Na śocati na kāṅkṣati: he is never disturbed about material necessities. Samaḥ sarveṣu bhūteṣu: he is ready to bestow blessings upon everyone equally. Mad-bhaktiṁ labhate parām: then he becomes a Vaiṣṇava. In this age, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura introduced the sacred thread ceremony for his Vaiṣṇava disciples, with the idea that people should understand that when one becomes a Vaiṣṇava he has already acquired the qualifications of a brāhmaṇa. Therefore in the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, those who are twice initiated so as to become brāhmaṇas must bear in mind their great responsibility to be truthful, control the mind and senses, be tolerant, and so on. Then their life will be successful. It was such brāhmaṇas that Nanda Mahārāja invited to chant the Vedic hymns, not ordinary brāhmaṇas. Verse 13 distinctly mentions hiṁsā-māna. The word māna refers to false prestige or false pride. Those who were falsely proud, thinking that they were brāhmaṇas because they were born in brāhmaṇa families, were never invited by Nanda Mahārāja on such occasions. Verse 14 mentions pavitrauṣadhi. In any ritualistic ceremony, many herbs and leaves were required. These were known as pavitra-patra. Sometimes there were nimba leaves, sometimes bael leaves, mango leaves, aśvattha leaves or āmalakī leaves. Similarly, there were pañca-gavya, pañca-śasya and pañca-ratna. Although Nanda Mahārāja belonged to the vaiśya community, everything was known to him. The most important word in these verses is mahā-guṇam, indicating that the brāhmaṇas were offered very palatable food of exalted quality. Such palatable dishes were generally prepared with two things, namely food grains and milk products. Bhagavad-gītā (18.44) therefore enjoins that human society must give protection to the cows and encourage agriculture ( kṛṣi-go-rakṣya-vāṇijyaṁ vaiśya-karma svabhāvajam ). Simply by expert cooking, hundreds and thousands of palatable dishes can be prepared from agricultural produce and milk products. This is indicated here by the words annaṁ mahā-guṇam. Still today in India, from these two things, namely food grains and milk, hundreds and thousands of varieties of food are prepared, and then they are offered to the Supreme Personality of Godhead. ( Catur-vidha-śrī-bhagavat-prasāda. Patraṁ puṣpaṁ phalaṁ toyaṁ yo me bhaktyā prayacchati. ) Then the prasāda is distributed. Even today in Jagannātha-kṣetra and other big temples, very palatable dishes are offered to the Deity, and prasāda is distributed profusely. Cooked by first-class brāhmaṇas with expert knowledge and then distributed to the public, this prasāda is also a blessing from the brāhmaṇas or Vaiṣṇavas. There are four kinds of prasāda ( catur-vidha ). Salty, sweet, sour and pungent tastes are made with different types of spices, and the food is prepared in four divisions, called carvya, cūṣya, lehya and peya — prasāda that is chewed, prasāda that is licked, prasāda tasted with the tongue, and prasāda that is drunk. Thus there are many varieties of prasāda, prepared very nicely with grains and ghee, offered to the Deity and distributed to the brāhmaṇas and Vaiṣṇavas and then to the general public. This is the way of human society. Killing the cows and spoiling the land will not solve the problem of food. This is not civilization. Uncivilized men living in the jungle and being unqualified to produce food by agriculture and cow protection may eat animals, but a perfect human society advanced in knowledge must learn how to produce first-class food simply by agriculture and protection of cows.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    When brāhmaṇas are free from envy, untruthfulness, unnecessary pride, grudges, disturbance by the opulence of others, and false prestige, their blessings never go in vain. Considering this, Nanda Mahārāja soberly took Kṛṣṇa on his lap and invited such truthful brāhmaṇas to perform a ritualistic ceremony according to the holy hymns of the Sāma Veda, Ṛg Veda and Yajur Veda. Then, while the hymns were being chanted, he bathed the child with water mixed with pure herbs, and after performing a fire ceremony, he sumptuously fed all the brāhmaṇas with first-class grains and other food.
    Commentary

    Knowing that the blessings of brahmanas were good for his child’s welfare, Nanda spoke this verse. Mana means pride. The blessings of qualified brahmanas must bear fruit. Krsna was sprinkled with water mixed with sarvausadhi and mahausadhi, purified by mantras from the sama, rg and yajur Vedas. He performed sacrifice and then fed the brahmanas with tasty aromatic food (mahagunan).

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Like Yaśodā, Nanda also performed actions using other people. This is described in four verses. First three verses are explained together. The first verse describes the excellent position of the brāhmaṇas. With absence of hatred, untruthfulness, deceit and malice, a person becomes inclined to dharma. By absence of violence and pride a person becomes inclined to liberation. It is said satyaṁ ca sama-darśanam: truth is seeing all beings equally. (SB 11.19.37) As well, in defining the Vaiṣṇava it is said: nārāyaṇa-parāḥ sarve na kutaścana bibhyati svargāpavarga-narakeṣv api tulyārtha-darśinaḥ Devotees solely engaged in the service of Nārāyaṇa never fear any condition of life. For them the heavenly planets, liberation and the hellish planets are all the same. SB 6.17.28 The brāhmaṇas are described as satya-śīlānām. Because to the meaning of the two verses quoted, it should be understood that these brāhmaṇas were the highest Vaiṣṇavas. Therefore this quality, being the best, is mentioned separately. They bathed the child while chanting mantras from the Yajur, Ṛg and Sāma Vedas, using water mixed with sarvauṣadhi and mahāuṣadhi herbs. Annam mahad-guṇam means rice with good taste and fragrance.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Like Yaśodā, Nanda also performed actions by employing other people. This is described in four verses. First three verses are explained together. Asūyā means to find faults in the innocent. Anṛtam means speaking what is false. These are verbal faults, since speech predominates in them. Dambha means to cheat others. This action is a bodily fault. Īrṣyā means intolerance, inability to tolerate others’ opinions. This is mental. Hiṁṣā is causing pain to others. Māna is pride. These manifest in words, body and mind. Though qualities like lust and anger are also faults, by removing these four faults all other faults are also removed. Or because they are all included in hiṁsā they are not mentioned. In the absence of these four, generally there is adherence to dharma. By absence of violence and pride a person becomes inclined to liberation. Satya-śīlānām means they have qualities like the Lord’s. Or they naturally worship the Lord. They are the highest devotees. Since this is the best quality it is mentioned last. While chanting Vedic mantras the brāhmaṇas bathed the child in water mixed with sarvauṣadhi and mahāuṣadhi. These herbs are mentioned in Hayaśīrṣa-paṣcarātraam. Sarvauṣadhi consists of muṛa, māṁṣī, vacā, kuṣṭam, śaileyam, rajanī of two types, śacī, campaka and mustam. Mahāuṣadhi consists of sahadevī, vacā, vyābhrī, balā, atibalā, śaṅkhapuṣpi, siṁhī and sūryāvartā. Brāhmaṇas chanted puṇyāhavācana (svastyanam). Nanda-gopaḥ indicates that Nanda was a king of the cowherds. This shows respect for him and that he was knowledgeable of all these rites. He performed the rites without fault, with faith (samāhitaḥ). Since he was the best vaiśya he offered oblations into the fire along with the brāhmaṇas. He fed them high quality rice with good aroma and taste.

    Related values & verses
    Related values

      His Holiness Haladhar Swami· 2020-07-13· Oriya

      His Holiness Haladhar Swami· 2020-07-15· Oriya
    Personal Guidance
    Nanda Mahārāja, even with Kṛṣṇa on his lap, sought blessings from truthful, envy-free brāhmaṇas — true protection comes through qualified Vaiṣṇava grace, not status.
    Practical Applications
    • Before any major undertaking today, seek the blessing of one person whose life shows truthfulness and freedom from envy — not someone merely senior in title.
    • Audit your own conduct for the six disqualifications named here (asūyā, anṛta, dambha, īrṣā, hiṁsā, māna) and name aloud the one most active in you this week.
    • Cook or offer one meal today with care as if feeding a Vaiṣṇava — using grains and milk products — and refuse the lazy shortcut of treating food as mere fuel.
    Analogy

    A lamp lit from a flame that is itself smokeless gives clean light; a lamp lit from a smoking wick spreads soot. Blessings from brāhmaṇas free of asūyā, anṛta, dambha, īrṣā, hiṁsā, māna are the smokeless flame — Nanda Mahārāja seeks only that flame to light his son's path, even with Kṛṣṇa already on his lap.

    Stories
    Vasiṣṭha's blessings for Rāma

    Daśaratha did not raise Rāma alone; he placed the prince in Vasiṣṭha's hands, and later in Viśvāmitra's. Truthful, envy-free brāhmaṇas shaped Rāma's gurukula and accompanied every major saṁskāra of His life (Rāmāyaṇa, Bāla-kāṇḍa).

    Nanda Mahārāja follows the same dharma in Vraja. The Lord on the lap does not exempt the household from seeking qualified brāhmaṇa blessings; rather, the Lord's presence makes those blessings more potent, not less necessary.

    Haridāsa Ṭhākura's free-of-envy speech

    When the Kazi struck Haridāsa Ṭhākura in twenty-two marketplaces, Haridāsa prayed for the Kazi's deliverance rather than his punishment (CC Antya 3). Free of asūyā, anṛta, dambha, īrṣā, hiṁsā, māna — the six exact disqualifications named in this verse — his blessings indeed never went in vain.

    Gauḍīya hagiography offers the perfect commentary on the verse's list: this is what the brāhmaṇa-disposition looks like in Kali-yuga, embodied in a Vaiṣṇava.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.7.16

    గావ: సర్వగుణోపేతా వాస:స్రగ్రుక్‍మమాలినీ: । ఆత్మజాభ్యుదయార్థాయ ప్రాదాత్తే చాన్వయుఞ్జత ॥ ౧౬ ॥

    Important Words
    • sarva-guṇa-upetāḥ — fully qualified with all good qualities
      Sarva (all) + guṇa (qualities) + upeta (endowed). Vaiṣṇava charity is graded — the cow given should be the first-class one, not the substandard. Prabhupāda often emphasized: Kṛṣṇa is satisfied by quality of bhakti, not quantity; the same applies to dāna offered through Him.
    • rukma-mālinīḥ — with garlands of gold
      Rukma (gold) + mālā (garland). Vraja's wealth was real — Nanda was wealthy enough to gold-garland his charity-cows. The Gauḍīya frame: artha is to be used, not despised; it is purified by being routed through the Lord's servants.
    • ātmaja-abhyudaya-arthāya — for the purpose of his son's affluence
      Ātmaja (own-born — son) + abhyudaya (rising, welfare) + artha (for the sake of). Nanda's charity is performed with a specific saṅkalpa — for Kṛṣṇa's welfare. Vaiṣṇava dāna is not vague good-feeling; it is dedicated, named, directed. The saṅkalpa carries the gift.
    • anvayuñjata — accepted them (with blessings)
      From anu-yuj, to join, to unite. The brāhmaṇas do not merely accept — they yoke themselves to Nanda's saṅkalpa, joining their tapas to his offering. Vaiṣṇava charity is two-sided: the gift is one half; the recipient's blessing is the half that makes it yajña.
    Nanda Mahārāja, for the sake of the affluence of his own son Kṛṣṇa, gave the brāhmaṇas cows fully decorated with garments, flower garlands and gold necklaces. These cows, fully qualified to give ample milk, were given to the brāhmaṇas in charity, and the brāhmaṇas accepted them and bestowed blessings upon the whole family, and especially upon Kṛṣṇa.
    Purport

    Nanda Mahārāja first fed the brāhmaṇas sumptuously and then gave them in charity first-class cows fully decorated with golden necklaces, garments and flower garlands.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Nanda Mahārāja, for the sake of the affluence of his own son Kṛṣṇa, gave the brāhmaṇas cows fully decorated with garments, flower garlands and gold necklaces. These cows, fully qualified to give ample milk, were given to the brāhmaṇas in charity, and the brāhmaṇas accepted them and bestowed blessings upon the whole family, and especially upon Kṛṣṇa.
    Commentary

    He gave cows. They are described as sarvagunopetah , endowed with all qualities. That means they gave much milk. The brahmanas then (anu) accepted the cows (ayunjata).

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The cows are described half the verse. He gave cows in order to produce prosperity for his son after destroying all disturbances, but this was only a symptom of his affection for his son, which was his goal in life. After taking their meal (anu), the brāhmaṇas gave blessings since this would naturally follow (though it not mentioned explicitly in the verse).

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    He gave the best cows fully ornamented, that gave abundant milk and were young, as gifts, for his son’s bodily health (abhyudaya) and dharma, artha etc. or for wealth (abhyudaya). Actual Kṛṣṇa himself is all puruṣārthas. Thus out of affection Nanda performed these actions. After eating (anu) the brāhmaṇas accepted gifts.

    Personal Guidance
    Nanda Mahārāja's charity to brāhmaṇas for Kṛṣṇa's welfare shows that wealth is purified when it flows toward the Lord through His devotees.
    Practical Applications
    • Today, give something tangible — money, food, or time — to a Vaiṣṇava or brāhmaṇa specifically as an offering for the welfare of someone you love.
    • Refuse to hoard your 'best' things; identify one first-class item you've been saving and give it in charity rather than letting aparigraha curdle into possessiveness.
    • Before any act of generosity, name the recipient's spiritual standing and the dependent you're offering it for, so the gift becomes yajña rather than transaction.
    Analogy

    A father who wants his child to thrive does not buy the child a toy but pours water into the roots of the family tree — strengthening the soil under everyone. Nanda's gold-decorated cows given to brāhmaṇas are water poured at the roots: Kṛṣṇa's affluence flows from the soil that nourishes His servants.

    Stories
    Rantideva's last meal

    Rantideva, after fasting forty-eight days, gave away his only meal to a hungry guest — and then the next portion to a still hungrier one, and then the water to a thirsty caṇḍāla (SB 9.21). His charity was not strategic; it was the natural overflow of one who saw Bhagavān in every recipient.

    Nanda's charity is the householder version of Rantideva's: not the last meal, but the first-class cows; not stripped of family, but offered for his son. The shared principle: wealth purifies by flowing toward Bhagavān through His devotees.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.7.17

    ↗
    విప్రా మన్త్రవిదో యుక్తాస్తైర్యా: ప్రోక్తాస్తథాశిష: । తా నిష్ఫలా భవిష్యన్తి న కదాచిదపి స్ఫుటమ్ ॥ ౧౭ ॥
    viprā mantra-vido yuktās
    [vipraa mantra-vido yuktaas]
    tair yāḥ proktās tathāśiṣaḥ
    [tair yaah proktaas tathaashishah]
    tā niṣphalā bhaviṣyanti
    [taa nishphalaa bhavishyanti]
    na kadācid api sphuṭam
    [na kadaachid api sphutam]
    Word-for-word
    viprāḥ — the brāhmaṇas mantra - vidaḥ — completely expert in chanting the Vedic hymns yuktāḥ — perfect mystic yogīs taiḥ — by them yāḥ — whatsoever proktāḥ — was spoken tathā — becomes just so āśiṣaḥ — all blessings tāḥ — such words niṣphalāḥ — useless, without fruit bhaviṣyanti na — never will become kadācit — at any time api — indeed sphuṭam — always factual, as it is .
    Blended reading (Sanskrit + English inline)
    viprā mantra-vido yuktās

    the brāhmaṇas

    completely expert in chanting the Vedic hymns

    sphuṭam

    Important Words
    • mantra-vidaḥ — expert in chanting Vedic hymns
      Mantra (sacred sound) + vid (knower). Mantra-vit is not one who has memorized the syllables but one who knows how mantra works — its connection to the deity, its timing, its śuddhi. Caitanya's translation of this principle to Kali-yuga: name-vit, one who knows that nāma and nāmī are non-different.
    • yuktāḥ — perfect mystic yogīs
      Yukta — yoked, joined. The same root as yoga. A yukta-brāhmaṇa is one already in union with the Lord through tapas; his mantra is no longer mere sound but his own yoga extended outward. Compare Gītā's yukta-vairāgya — Rūpa Gosvāmī's principle of action yoked to Kṛṣṇa.
    • āśiṣaḥ — blessings
      From āśās, to wish, to bless. An āśīr is a blessing-formula, formally pronounced. The verse insists it is never fruitless when spoken by a yukta — a strong claim. Gauḍīya commentaries extend: the guru's āśīr at dīkṣā is the seed of all later realization.
    • niṣphalāḥ — useless, without fruit
      Nir (without) + phala (fruit). The vocabulary of phala — fruit, result — recurs across the Gītā (karma-phala-tyāga) and Bhāgavatam. The verse says the brāhmaṇa-yogī's blessings are na niṣphalāḥ kadācit — never fruitless ever. The doubled negation is emphatic.
    The brāhmaṇas, who were completely expert in chanting the Vedic hymns, were all yogīs fully equipped with mystic powers. Whatever blessings they spoke were certainly never fruitless.
    Purport

    Brāhmaṇas fully equipped with the brahminical qualifications are always yogīs fully powerful in mystic yoga. Their words never fail. In every transaction with other members of society, brāhmaṇas are certainly dependable. In this age, however, one must take into account that the brāhmaṇas are uncertain in their qualifications. Because there are no yajñic brāhmaṇas, all yajñas are forbidden. The only yajña recommended in this age is saṅkīrtana-yajña. Yajñaiḥ saṅkīrtana-prāyair yajanti hi sumedhasaḥ ( Bhāg. 11.5.32 ). Yajña is meant to satisfy Viṣṇu ( yajñārthāt karmaṇo ’nyatra loko ’yaṁ karma-bandhanaḥ ). Because in this age there are no qualified brāhmaṇas, people should perform yajña by chanting the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra ( yajñaiḥ saṅkīrtana-prāyair yajanti hi sumedhasaḥ ). Life is meant for yajña, and yajña is performed by the chanting of Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    The brāhmaṇas, who were completely expert in chanting the Vedic hymns, were all yogīs fully equipped with mystic powers. Whatever blessings they spoke were certainly never fruitless.
    Commentary

    The brahmanas are described here as yukta which means yoginah (yogis), and thus, whatever blessings they gave must come true.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Who were these brāhmaṇas? They knew the meaning of the Vedas (veda-vidaḥ). Another version has mantra-vidaḥ (knowers of mantras). That is an indication of their learning. They were devotees of the Lord (yuktāḥ), since the Vedas indicate prema: bhagavān brahma kārtsnyena trir anvīkṣya manīṣayā | tad adhyavasyat kūṭa-stho ratir ātman yato bhavet || The unchanging Lord reviewed three times the Vedas completely with his intelligence and determined that process which produces prema in the self. SB 2.2.34 Bhagavad Gītā 6.47 says śraddhāvān bhajate yo māṁ sa me yuktatamo mataḥ: he who worships me with faith is the greatest devotee (yuktatamaḥ). Thus when they gave blessings, the results were certain. Following the desire of the brāhmaṇas, those blessings would be manifested in Kṛṣṇa at the appropriate time.

    SB 10.7.16 SB 10.7.18 SB 10.7.19 SB 10.7.20 BG 7.8 BG 5.4 BG 14.8 BG 15.7 BG 4.1 BG 14.3
    Concepts
    Mantra
    Related lectures
    • Manonatha Pr SB 10-07-17 - 2011-07-14
      His Grace Manonatha Prabhu· 2011-07-14· English
    • PrahladAnS SB 10-07-17 - Tapa Means Fired Up - 1997-06-21 Radhadesh
      His Holiness Prahladananada Swami· 1997-06-21· English
    Personal Guidance
    Words from a qualified brāhmaṇa never fail; in this age, take shelter of saṅkīrtana — the only yajña whose blessings still bear fruit.
    Practical Applications
    • Chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa mahā-mantra audibly today as your personal yajña, trusting it carries the same potency the Vedic brāhmaṇas wielded.
    • Refuse to seek blessings from self-styled gurus and astrologers; instead, seek the saṅga of devotees whose words are anchored in śāstra and Kṛṣṇa.
    • Notice when you speak casually or carelessly, and resolve to make your speech truthful and Kṛṣṇa-centered, since a sādhaka's words gain weight only through tapas and chanting.
    Analogy

    A king's well-trained heralds proclaim his decree in the marketplace, and what they speak inevitably comes to pass — not because the heralds are powerful, but because the king stands behind every word. The brāhmaṇa-yogīs' blessings carry the same authority because Kṛṣṇa stands behind the mantra; in Kali-yuga, the same authority lives in the holy name.

    Stories
    Nāmācārya Haridāsa Ṭhākura

    Haridāsa Ṭhākura chanted three lakhs of holy names daily and was given by Caitanya Mahāprabhu the title nāmācārya — the ācārya of the holy name (CC Antya 11). In Kali-yuga, when the brāhmaṇa-yogīs of 10.7.17 are rare, Haridāsa demonstrated that the same potency is available through harināma.

    The verse here points to the Vedic past; Caitanya's siddhānta extends it to the present. The mantra that never fails is now Hare Kṛṣṇa, and the qualified chanter is anyone who takes the holy name seriously.

    📝 My note

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.7.18

    ↗
    ఏకదారోహమారూఢం లాలయన్తీ సుతం సతీ । గరిమాణం శిశోర్వోఢుం న సేహే గిరికూటవత్ ॥ ౧౮ ॥
    ekadāroham ārūḍhaṁ
    [ekadaaroham aaroodham]
    lālayantī sutaṁ satī
    [laalayantee sutam satee]

    [garimaanam shishor vodhum]
    na sehe giri-kūṭavat
    [na sehe giri-kootavat]
    Word-for-word
    ekadā — one time (estimated to have been when Kṛṣṇa was one year old) āroham — on His mother’s lap ārūḍham — who was sitting lālayantī — was patting sutam — her son satī — mother Yaśodā garimāṇam — because of an increase in heaviness śiśoḥ — of the child voḍhum — to bear Him na — not sehe — was able giri - kūṭa - vat — appearing like the weight of a mountain peak .

    na
    not
    sehe
    was able
    giri - kūṭa - vat
    appearing like the weight of a mountain peak .
    Important Words
    • garimāṇam — increase in heaviness
      From garu (heavy) — same root as guru (heavy with knowledge). Garimā is one of the eight aṣṭa-siddhis — the yogic power to become heavy at will. Kṛṣṇa, source of all siddhis, manifests garimā for a moment to dismantle Yaśodā's bodily concept that He is small.
    • giri-kūṭa-vat — like the weight of a mountain peak
      Giri (mountain) + kūṭa (peak) + vat (like). The same Kṛṣṇa who later lifts Govardhana with one finger here cannot be lifted by His mother. The reversal is structurally Vaiṣṇava: aiśvarya and mādhurya occupy the same body, switching by Yoga-māyā's arrangement.
    • voḍhum — to bear Him
      Infinitive of vah — to carry. Same root as vāhana (vehicle, carrier). The verse's verb hints at the deepest theology: Kṛṣṇa is the One who carries (jagad-vāhaka), never the one carried. When Yaśodā holds Him, He is allowing the holding; when He withdraws permission, no one bears Him.
    • satī — the chaste / virtuous (mother Yaśodā)
      Satī — true, real, virtuous; the feminine of sat. Repeatedly applied to Yaśodā in this chapter. The Gauḍīya frame: vātsalya-rasa requires the devotee's chastity-toward-Kṛṣṇa, the refusal to relocate maternal feeling onto any other object. Yaśodā is satī because Kṛṣṇa alone is her son.
    One day, a year after Kṛṣṇa’s appearance, mother Yaśodā was patting her son on her lap. But suddenly she felt the child to be heavier than a mountain peak, and she could no longer bear His weight.
    Purport

    Lālayantī. Sometimes a mother lifts her child, and when the child falls in her hands, the child laughs, and the mother also enjoys pleasure. Yaśodā used to do this, but this time Kṛṣṇa became very heavy, and she could not bear His weight. Under the circumstances, it is to be understood that Kṛṣṇa was aware of the coming of Tṛṇāvartāsura, who would take Him far away from His mother. Kṛṣṇa knew that when Tṛṇāvarta came and took Him away from His mother’s lap, mother Yaśodā would be greatly bereaved. He did not want His mother to suffer any difficulty from the demon. Therefore, because He is the source of everything ( janmādy asya yataḥ ), He assumed the heaviness of the entire universe. The child was on the lap of Yaśodā, who was therefore in possession of everything in the world, but when the child assumed such heaviness, she had to put Him down in order to give Tṛṇāvartāsura an opportunity to take Him away and play with Him for some time before the child returned to the lap of His mother.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    One day, a year after Kṛṣṇa's appearance, mother Yaśodā was patting her son on her lap. But suddenly she felt the child to be heavier than a mountain peak, and she could no longer bear His weight.
    Commentary

    "What use are all these things for me, the Lord of all wealth?" When Krsna was one year old this event took place. Bhagavatam 10. 26.6. says that when he was one year old, Trnavarta took away Krsna. Placing Krsna on her lap and lifing him up in the air with her two hands Yasoda became blissful. Krsna then became heavy like a mountain top. Yasoda was not able to carry him. Knowing that Trnavarta was about to take away Yasoda along with himself, and wanting to avoid pain to Yasoda, Krsna displayed his aisvarya sakti and became heavy, so that she put him on the ground. "Yasoda can only lift me a little, but I want to play in the sky." Desiring like this, Krsna let the asura Trnavarta approach.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    “While living in Vraja I do not deserve to be covered up or restricted at all.” Thinking in this way, Kṛṣṇa desired to go into the sky. When he was one year old (ekadā), this event occurred, since it will be said later: eka-hāyana āsīno hriyamāṇo vihāyasā daityena yas tṛṇāvartam ahan kaṇṭha-grahāturam At the age of one, while sitting peacefully he was taken up into the sky by the demon Tṛṇāvarta. But baby Kṛṣṇa grabbed the demon’s neck, causing him great pain, and thus killed him. SB 10.26.6 Yaśodā cared for him, kissing him and giving him her breast. Sometimes she lifted him in her arms playfully. She was knowledgeable (satī) of how to care for him. She was not able to carry him by her own strength. She was alone at the time. Later a similar condition is described: ekadā gṛha-dāsīṣu yaśodā nanda-gehinī karmāntara-niyuktāsu nirmamantha svayaṁ dadhi One day when mother Yaśodā saw that all the maidservants were engaged in other household affairs, she personally began to churn the yogurt. SB 10.9.1

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    “While living in Vraja I do not deserve to be covered up or restricted at all.” Thinking in this way, Kṛṣṇa desired to go into the sky. When he was one year old (ekadā), this event occurred, since it will be said later: eka-hāyana āsīno hriyamāṇo vihāyasā daityena yas tṛṇāvartam ahan kaṇṭha-grahāturam At the age of one, while sitting peacefully he was taken up into the sky by the demon Tṛṇāvarta. But baby Kṛṣṇa grabbed the demon’s neck, causing him great pain, and thus killed him. SB 10.26.6 Though Kṛṣṇa who reveals at all times his unlimited nature as bhagavān has other attractive pastimes during this period, which are well known to the people of Mathurā, such as defeating the demon disguised as a brāhmaṇa named Śrīdhara, these pastimes are not related since they are not well known. Or Śukdeva could not tell all these unlimited pastimes in the short seven days that Parīkṣit had left of his life. Children generally begin to walk when they are one year old, if they are strong. Before crawling, one astonishing pastime is mentioned. Or Śukadeva simply relates pastimes with increasing sweetness. Sometimes there is deviation from this, on considerations of chronology. Generally the events related are not strictly chronological. That will be seen in various places. Yaśodā cared for him, kissing him and giving him her breast. Sometimes she lifted him in her arms playfully. She was knowledgeable (satī) of how to care for him. He became heavier that a mountain of rice or heavier than the gold peak of Meru, or heavier than all mountains. She could not lift him. Since she always carried him with affection why he had become so heavy at this time? Or though he is by nature heavier than countless universes, for the devotees he is light. In order to kill Tṛnāvarta, he manifested greater heaviness so that she would take him from her lap. Or Yaśodā’s power to carry him disappeared.

    • Vrindavan Chandra Das Goswami SB 10-07-18 Hindi - 2020-08-06 ISKCON Vrindavan
      His Holiness Vrindavan Chandra Das Goswami· 2020-08-06· Hindi
    Personal Guidance
    Kṛṣṇa quietly bears the universe's weight to shield His devotee from grief — He arranges every difficulty before it touches those who love Him.
    Practical Applications
    • Notice one moment today when a heaviness lifted from your life unexpectedly, and offer silent thanks to Kṛṣṇa for the protection you did not see arranged.
    • Speak to someone you love about a fear you carry for them, and then formally entrust that fear to Kṛṣṇa instead of trying to control the outcome yourself.
    • Refuse to measure Kṛṣṇa's care by visible comfort — when something feels suddenly 'too heavy,' read it as His preparation, not His absence.
    Analogy

    A toddler who has fallen asleep in his father's arms feels, in the moment of waking, the full weight of the man's chest — the same chest that was bearing him gently a moment ago. Yaśodā feels Kṛṣṇa's weight the same way: the universe-bearing Lord, who normally hides His mass within Yoga-māyā, allows His mother a single instant to feel what she is actually holding.

    Stories
    Bhīma lifting his mother and brothers

    After the lac-house, Bhīma carried his mother Kuntī and four brothers on his shoulders and ran for miles through the forest (MBh Ādi-parva 145). Yaśodā, asked to carry Kṛṣṇa for a moment, cannot — because the One she holds is heavier than what Bhīma carried in all his lifetimes.

    The contrast is exactly the verse's point: even the strongest devotee can carry only what Bhagavān allows him to carry. Yaśodā's failure to lift Kṛṣṇa is His teaching, not her weakness.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.7.19

    ↗
    భూమౌ నిధాయ తం గోపీ విస్మితా భారపీడితా । మహాపురుషమాదధ్యౌ జగతామాస కర్మసు ॥ ౧౯ ॥
    bhūmau nidhāya taṁ gopī

    vismitā bhāra-pīḍitā
    [vismitaa bhaara-peeditaa]

    bhūmau — on the ground nidhāya — placing tam — the child gopī — mother Yaśodā vismitā — being astonished bhāra - pīḍitā — being aggrieved by the weight of the child mahā - puruṣam — Lord Viṣṇu, Nārāyaṇa ādadhyau — took shelter of jagatām — as if the weight of the whole world āsa — engaged herself karmasu — in other household affairs .

    jagatām āsa karmasu
    jagatām

    āsa

    karmasu
    in other household affairs .
    Important Words
    • mahā-puruṣam — Lord Viṣṇu, Nārāyaṇa
      Mahā (great) + puruṣa (Person). One of Viṣṇu's titles. The Bhāgavatam emphasizes that the Supreme is Mahā-puruṣa — a Person, not an impersonal principle. Yaśodā's mind, under stress, runs to a Person, not to brahman-as-abstraction — even though that Person is in fact her son.
    • ādadhyau — took shelter of (in meditation)
      From ā-dhyai, to meditate upon, to take into the heart. The verb names smaraṇa — Vaiṣṇava remembrance. Caitanya Mahāprabhu placed smaraṇa among the nine limbs of bhakti. In Yaśodā, smaraṇa erupts spontaneously under bhāra — the heart's reflex when the body's strength fails.
    • bhāra-pīḍitā — aggrieved by the weight
      Bhāra (weight, burden) + pīḍita (pressed, afflicted). Yaśodā's distress is physical, but the verse uses bhāra in a way the Gītā often uses it — as the weight of saṁsāra itself. The mother's bodily lesson mirrors the soul's situation: only when crushed by bhāra does smaraṇa awake.
    • vismitā — being astonished
      From vi-smṛ, literally 'to forget away' — but in standard usage, astonished, struck out of one's frame. Vismaya is the entry-rasa to adbhuta. Yaśodā's vismaya is the third in a chapter-long sequence (cart, weight, mouth) by which Yoga-māyā lifts and re-drops her vātsalya-veil.
    Feeling the child to be as heavy as the entire universe and therefore being anxious, thinking that perhaps the child was being attacked by some other ghost or demon, the astonished mother Yaśodā put the child down on the ground and began to think of Nārāyaṇa. Foreseeing disturbances, she called for the brāhmaṇas to counteract this heaviness, and then she engaged in her other household affairs. She had no alternative than to remember the lotus feet of Nārāyaṇa, for she could not understand that Kṛṣṇa was the original source of everything.
    Purport

    Mother Yaśodā did not understand that Kṛṣṇa is the heaviest of all heavy things and that Kṛṣṇa rests within everything ( mat-sthāni sarva-bhūtāni ). As confirmed in Bhagavad-gītā (9.4) , mayā tatam idaṁ sarvaṁ jagad avyakta-mūrtinā: Kṛṣṇa is everywhere in His impersonal form, and everything rests upon Him. Nonetheless, na cāhaṁ teṣv avasthitaḥ: Kṛṣṇa is not everywhere. Mother Yaśodā was unable to understand this philosophy because she was dealing with Kṛṣṇa as His real mother by the arrangement of Yoga-māyā. Not understanding the importance of Kṛṣṇa, she could only seek shelter of Nārāyaṇa for Kṛṣṇa’s safety and call the brāhmaṇas to counteract the situation.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Feeling the child to be as heavy as the entire universe and therefore being anxious, thinking that perhaps the child was being attacked by some other ghost or demon, the astonished mother Yaśodā put the child down on the ground and began to think of Nārāyaṇa. Foreseeing disturbances, she called for the brāhmaṇas to counteract this heaviness, and then she engaged in her other household affairs. She had no alternative than to remember the lotus feet of Nārāyaṇa, for she could not understand that Kṛṣṇa was the original source of everything.
    Commentary

    Disturbed, she placed Krsna on the ground. How has he become so heavy she wondered in dismay. Maybe some raksasa had done this. With this fear, she took shelter of Narayana, he Lord of the universe. Glancing upward to Vaikuntha, in meditation she said, " O Lord, You have given this son. You must protect him." Then in anxiety, she went to call some brahmanas to perform svastyayana karma.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The meaning of Śrīdhara Svāmī’s commentary is that Kṛṣṇa made himself heavy so that Yaśodā would put him down. Thus he avoided giving her injury. The word mṛtyu in his commentary cannot mean death but only injury, since Yaśodā cannot die. He explains that she was pained by the weight of the universe within his belly, since he possesses all powers. This power of containing the universe within himself is also established in the Dāmodara pastime, though he was tied up eventually. She placed him on the ground, fearing that the bed could not bear the weight. She could not bear the weight because of his manifestation of power. She surrendered to the Lord of the universe and engaged in auspicious rites such as svastyayana out of affection for him alone, for it is said yad-dhāmārtha-suhṛt-priyātma-tanaya-prāṇāśayās tvat-kṛte: so what is left for you to give these devotees of Vṛndāvana, whose homes, wealth, friends, dear relations, bodies, children and very lives and hearts are all dedicated only to you? (SB 10.14.35)

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Afflicted by his heaviness she placed him on the ground. She lost her smile (vismitāḥ), worrying about some danger. Or she became astonished at the sudden increase in weight. She remembered (ādadhyau) the lord of the universe or Nārāyaṇa who supports the weight of the universe, who came into her mind because of the extraordinary weight, in order to protect her son, since she was a cowherd woman, with natural affection for her son. Recovering after a short time, she engaged in household duties for her son.

    • VarsanaSw SB 10-07-19
      His Holiness Varsana Swami· English
    Personal Guidance
    Yaśodā cannot lift Kṛṣṇa because He carries the universe — when life turns unbearably heavy, the dhīra remembers the Lord rests on no one but Himself.
    Practical Applications
    • Notice the one burden today that feels heavier than it should, and instead of bracing harder, set it down and turn to Kṛṣṇa as Yaśodā turned to Nārāyaṇa.
    • Refuse the reflex of seeking only material counter-measures (the brāhmaṇas, the fix, the workaround) before first remembering the lotus feet of the Lord who already holds the weight.
    • Speak one honest sentence aloud today acknowledging that you are not the source — Kṛṣṇa is — and let that admission shape your next decision.
    Analogy

    When the lamp itself becomes heavier than the lamp-stand can bear, the wise householder does not strain the stand — she sets the lamp down and calls the priest. Yaśodā places Kṛṣṇa on the ground and turns to Nārāyaṇa: she does not yet know that the Lord on the ground and the Lord she remembers are one.

    Stories
    Draupadī's call to Govinda

    When Duḥśāsana began to disrobe her in the Kuru assembly, Draupadī first tried to hold the sari herself. Only when her arms failed did she raise both hands and cry, govinda dvārakā-vāsin kṛṣṇa gopī-jana-priya — and the unending cloth descended (MBh Sabhā-parva).

    Yaśodā's instinct in 10.7.19 is the same shape: when material capacity fails, remember Nārāyaṇa. The verse names the moment in every devotee's life when self-effort is exhausted and surrender becomes the only remaining act.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.7.20

    దైత్యో నామ్నా తృణావర్త: కంసభృత్య: ప్రణోదిత: । చక్రవాతస్వరూపేణ జహారాసీనమర్భకమ్ ॥ ౨౦ ॥
    daityo nāmnā tṛṇāvartaḥ
    [daityo naamnaa trinaavartah]
    kaṁsa-bhṛtyaḥ praṇoditaḥ
    [kamsa-bhrityah pranoditah]
    cakravāta-svarūpeṇa
    [chakravaata-svaroopena]
    jahārāsīnam arbhakam
    [jahaaraaseenam arbhakam]
    Word-for-word
    daityaḥ — another demon nāmnā — by the name tṛṇāvartaḥ — Tṛṇāvartāsura kaṁsa - bhṛtyaḥ — a servant of Kaṁsa praṇoditaḥ — having been induced by him cakravāta - svarūpeṇa — in the form of a whirlwind jahāra — swept away āsīnam — the sitting arbhakam — child .
    Blended reading (Sanskrit + English inline)
    daityo nāmnā tṛṇāvartaḥ

    in the form of a whirlwind
    jahārāsīnam arbhakam
    jahāra
    swept away
    āsīnam
    the sitting
    arbhakam
    child .
    Important Words
    • daityaḥ — demon
      Daitya — descendant of Diti, mother of the asuras. Daityas and asuras differ in genealogy but share function in the līlā: they are forces sent to test, threaten, and be vanquished. Vaiṣṇava theology reads them as Yoga-māyā's instruments for displaying Kṛṣṇa's protective power.
    • kaṁsa-bhṛtyaḥ — a servant of Kaṁsa
      Kaṁsa (the asura-king) + bhṛtya (servant, dependent). Tṛṇāvarta does not act on his own — he is dispatched. The Gauḍīya frame surfaces the chain: behind every individual demon is a Kaṁsa-tattva, the organized envious power; behind every Kaṁsa is the Lord's permission for the līlā to unfold.
    • cakravāta-svarūpeṇa — in the form of a whirlwind
      Cakra (wheel) + vāta (wind) + svarūpa (own form). The whirlwind is Tṛṇāvarta's assumed form; svarūpa here means his disguise, not his true essence. Compare with Kṛṣṇa, whose svarūpa is sac-cid-ānanda — never a disguise. The contrast is the chapter's chief teaching.
    • jahāra — swept away
      From hṛ — to take, to carry off. Same root as harāmi in Gītā 16.19 (where Kṛṣṇa says He casts asuras into demoniac wombs). Tṛṇāvarta thinks he is hṛ-ing Kṛṣṇa; in fact he is being hṛ-ed by Him. The verb cuts both ways.
    While the child was sitting on the ground, a demon named Tṛṇāvarta, who was a servant of Kaṁsa’s, came there as a whirlwind, at Kaṁsa’s instigation, and very easily carried the child away into the air.
    Purport

    Kṛṣṇa’s heaviness was unbearable for the child’s mother, but when Tṛṇāvartāsura came, he immediately carried the child away. This was another demonstration of Kṛṣṇa’s inconceivable energy. When the Tṛṇāvarta demon came, Kṛṣṇa became lighter than the grass so that the demon could carry Him away. This was ānanda-cinmaya-rasa, Kṛṣṇa’s blissful, transcendental pleasure.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    While the child was sitting on the ground, a demon named Tṛṇāvarta, who was a servant of Kaṁsa's, came there as a whirlwind, at Kaṁsa's instigation, and very easily carried the child away into the air.
    Commentary

    Just at that time, Trnavarta came and stole the child. By the Lord’s aisvarya sakti, at that time Krsna became light so that Trnavarta could lift him.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Two verses describe the demon. As a whirlwind demon, he was naturally destructive, very powerful and without counter measure. Since he was a servant of Kaṁsa, he was most inimical. He was instigated to come by Kaṁsa. Previously he sent with an ordinary form Pūtanā as a baby killer. Kaṁsa became worried when she was killed, even though she disguised herself. He then sent Śakaṭa, who did not appear with any form. When he was killed, Kaṁsa became greatly frightened. He then sent the whirlwind demon who was with form and without form, very strong and impossible to grasp. But later Kṛṣṇa squeezed his throat and choked him. Kṛṣṇa was sitting down, but within the sight of Yaśodā, for on seeing the demon she became astonished and full of lamentation. Kṛṣṇa appeared to be a small child (arbhakam) but was hiding his great powers.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Tṛnāvarta means “he who turns the world upside down like grass” or “he who by his speedy force makes grass whirl about.” This indicates that he was very strong. Since he was Kaṁsa’s servant, he was very evil. He was instigated by Kaṁsa. Kaṁsa previously gave him instructions. Or he was sent at this time to do violence after the news of Pūtana’s killing was heard. Kṛṣṇa was seated, pleasing his mother who feared he would go somewhere. Or he was seated, appearing as a small child, hiding his powers, in order that Tṛnāvarta would take him fearlessly.

    Related values & verses

      His Holiness Haladhar Swami· 2020-07-20· Oriya
    Personal Guidance
    Kṛṣṇa makes Himself heavy or light by His own sweet will — surrender to His personal līlā, not to a force you can manage.
    Practical Applications
    • Notice today where Kṛṣṇa feels 'too heavy' to carry — a duty, a relationship, a sādhana — and recognize that weight as His personal arrangement, not your failure.
    • Refuse to reduce Kṛṣṇa to a metaphor or inner force; speak to Him by name in one concrete prayer this morning, asking to be carried as Yaśodā was relieved.
    • When a sudden 'whirlwind' (an unexpected loss, demand, or interruption) sweeps in today, pause and offer that moment to Kṛṣṇa instead of bracing against it.
    Analogy

    A child sets a paper boat afloat on the courtyard puddle, and a sudden wind picks it up and carries it skyward — except the boat is sailing the wind, not the wind the boat. Tṛṇāvarta seems to carry Kṛṣṇa off, but every gust the demon raises is the breath Kṛṣṇa has already permitted; the abduction is the abductor's last journey.

    Stories
    Rāvaṇa carrying Sītā

    Rāvaṇa, like Tṛṇāvarta, succeeded in 'carrying off' the Lord's beloved — and yet that success was the seed of his ruin. Sītā in Aśoka-vana, like Kṛṣṇa in Tṛṇāvarta's whirlwind, was never out of the Lord's arrangement.

    Gauḍīya commentators read both episodes the same way: the demon's apparent victory is the līlā by which the Lord brings the demon to his appointed end. Surrender does not protect from being seized; it ensures the seizing destroys the seizer, not the surrendered.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.7.21

    గోకులం సర్వమావృణ్వన్ ముష్ణంశ్చక్షూంషి రేణుభి: । ఈరయన్ సుమహాఘోరశబ్దేన ప్రదిశో దిశ: ॥ ౨౧ ॥

    Important Words
    • gokulam — the whole tract of land known as Gokula
      Go (cow) + kula (community). Gokula is the cow-people's village, Kṛṣṇa's first dhāma. The Gauḍīya frame: Gokula on earth is non-different from Goloka above (Brahma-saṁhitā). Tṛṇāvarta's attack on Gokula is therefore an attack on the dhāma itself, not merely on a village.
    • cakṣūṁṣi — the power of vision
      Plural of cakṣus (eye). The demon's targeted faculty is sight — the very faculty the gopas need to find Kṛṣṇa. Māyā's standard work, per the Gītā (7.13), is to make the jīva unable to see the Lord though He is everywhere. Tṛṇāvarta's dust is māyā's literal version.
    • reṇubhiḥ — by particles of dust
      Reṇu — dust, particle. The Bhāgavatam elsewhere praises the reṇu of the Lord's feet and the reṇu of the bhakta's feet as supremely auspicious. Tṛṇāvarta's reṇu is the demoniac inversion — dust that blinds rather than reveals. Same substance, opposite work.
    • su-mahā-ghora — very fierce and terrible
      Su (very) + mahā (great) + ghora (fearful). The demoniac is associated with ghora in the Bhāgavatam — the same word used for the hellish planets. Tṛṇāvarta's sound is structurally hell-noise erupting into Vraja. Yet Vraja is unbreakable; the noise dies, the Lord remains.
    Covering the whole land of Gokula with particles of dust, that demon, acting as a strong whirlwind, covered everyone’s vision and began vibrating everywhere with a greatly fearful sound.
    Purport

    Tṛṇāvartāsura assumed the form of a whirlwind and covered with a dust storm the whole tract of land known as Gokula, so that no one could see even the nearest thing.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Covering the whole land of Gokula with particles of dust, that demon, acting as a strong whirlwind, covered everyone's vision and began vibrating everywhere with a greatly fearful sound.
    Commentary

    A tumultuous sound reverberated (irayan) in all directions.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    He covered the place with dust. Otherwise he could not steal the child. He also took away the power of vision from the people and made a great sound in all directions.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The demon covered Gokula with dust, and made all eyes close. Or covering Gokula with darkness he closed all eyes with dust in order to cheat Yāśodā’s vision. He made loud sounds in all directions. Fearing that the child would cry, he made sounds of wind so that no one could hear the crying.

    Related values & verses
    Related values

      His Grace Tamohara Prabhu· 2012-04-06· English

      His Holiness Bir Krishna Goswami· 2019-05-15· English

      His Holiness Haladhar Swami· 2020-07-21· Oriya
    Personal Guidance
    Māyā can blanket Gokula in dust and terror in a moment — only those sheltered in Kṛṣṇa keep their sight when the storm hides everything else.
    Practical Applications
    • Notice today the first 'dust storm' that obscures your discrimination — a news scroll, a quarrel, a craving — and step back to chant Kṛṣṇa's names before reacting.
    • Refuse to trust your own eyes when fear or noise rises; pause, remember that Kṛṣṇa is present in Gokula even now, and act from that shelter rather than from panic.
    • Identify one 'Tṛṇāvarta' in your week — a force that lifts you off the ground and spins you — and protect your sādhana time from it with a fixed, non-negotiable slot.
    Analogy

    A thief who knows he cannot fight the watchman in the lamplight first throws dust into every torch; in the sudden blindness, he tries to slip past. Tṛṇāvarta's dust-storm is exactly that — māyā's standard manoeuvre, blinding the household so that the demon may seize the Lord. But blindness is the demon's last weapon, not his first triumph.

    Stories
    Duryodhana's house of darkness

    In Hastināpura, Duryodhana ruled by the dust of intrigue — gambling-houses, lac-houses, poisons, courts thick with anṛta-vāda. His was Tṛṇāvarta-style governance: blind everyone, then take what you want.

    Kṛṣṇa's response was Kurukṣetra. The pattern is consistent across Bhāgavatam and Mahābhārata: the demoniac strategy is to manufacture darkness; the Lord's response is to be the lamp that the dust cannot extinguish.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.7.22

    ↗
    ముహూర్తమభవద్ గోష్ఠం రజసా తమసావృతమ్ । సుతం యశోదా నాపశ్యత్తస్మిన్ న్యస్తవతీ యత: ॥ ౨౨ ॥
    muhūrtam abhavad goṣṭhaṁ
    [muhoortam abhavad goshtham]
    rajasā tamasāvṛtam

    Important Words
    • muhūrtam — for a moment
      A muhūrta — roughly forty-eight minutes, but used here for a brief while. Vraja's blindness is timed; Kṛṣṇa's līlās of separation are always bounded. Gauḍīya commentaries emphasize: the Lord never withdraws from His pure devotee for more than a muhūrta — the apparent separation is fuel for deeper union.
    • rajasā — by big particles of dust
      Rajas — dust, but also the guṇa of passion. The double-meaning is exact: when rajas covers the senses, the soul cannot find the Lord just placed in front of it. Tṛṇāvarta is rajo-guṇa materialized as wind.
    • tamasā āvṛtam — covered with darkness
      Tamas — darkness, the guṇa of ignorance. Rajas + tamas together pile up across the verse. The Gītā's diagnosis (14.5–8) is precisely this — rajas agitates and tamas covers; their combined work is the inability to perceive the ever-present Lord.
    • nyastavatī — she had placed
      From ni-as, to set down. The crucial verb: she placed Him there. The Gauḍīya principle of nyāsa — placing — runs through all bhakti. When the storm clears, the Lord will be exactly where the devotee has nyāsa-ed Him; He does not leave. The fault, when seeing fails, is never with His position.
    For a moment, the whole pasturing ground was overcast with dense darkness from the dust storm, and mother Yaśodā was unable to find her son where she had placed Him.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    For a moment, the whole pasturing ground was overcast with dense darkness from the dust storm, and mother Yaśodā was unable to find her son where she had placed Him.
    Commentary

    She did not see him there (tasmin), where (yatah) she had left him (nyastavati).

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The place was covered by dust (rajasā) and consequent darkness (tamasā), so that the Lord could not be seen. This is comparable to the yogī’s heart covered by rajas and tamas, by taking a second meaning of rajas and tamas. When it is pure he can see the Lord and when covered by ignorance and passion he cannot see the Lord. She places her son by her side (tasmin) but could not see him because the place was covered with dust.

    • CMS SB 10-07-22-55 - 2016-10-21 Bhaktivedanta Manor
      His Holiness Candramauli Swami· 2016-10-21· English
    Personal Guidance
    When the dust storm of māyā suddenly blinds you and Kṛṣṇa seems lost from view, do not panic — He is exactly where you placed Him.
    Practical Applications
    • Notice the moments today when sudden agitation — rajas and tamas as a literal dust storm — obscures your remembrance of Kṛṣṇa, and pause until the air clears rather than acting from that blindness.
    • Refuse to conclude that Kṛṣṇa is absent simply because your senses cannot find Him; return to the place where you last 'set Him down' — your japa, your prasāda offering, your morning sādhana — and resume there.
    • Speak to Kṛṣṇa with the protective bhāva of Yaśodā today: stand guard over your remembrance of Him as a mother stands guard over her child in a storm.
    Analogy

    A diya placed on the windowsill of one's own house is sometimes invisible behind a sudden gust of curtain — but it has not gone anywhere; the curtain has merely settled between the eye and the flame. Yaśodā cannot see Kṛṣṇa, but He is precisely where she placed Him, only the dust hangs in between.

    Stories
    Arjuna's lost vision before the Viśva-rūpa

    Before granting Arjuna divya-cakṣus, Kṛṣṇa stood plainly visible in the chariot — and yet Arjuna could not see the Viśva-rūpa with his own eyes. The Lord was not absent; the vision had to be granted (Gītā 11.8).

    Yaśodā's blindness in 10.7.22 is the same kind: Kṛṣṇa has not moved, but seeing Him in this moment requires more than ordinary sight. The verse is the Bhāgavatam's quiet parallel to the Gītā's divya-cakṣus principle.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.7.23

    ↗
    నాపశ్యత్కశ్చనాత్మానం పరం చాపి విమోహిత: । తృణావర్తనిసృష్టాభి: శర్కరాభిరుపద్రుత: ॥ ౨౩ ॥

    Important Words
    • ātmānam — himself
      Ātman — the self. In Gauḍīya theology, jīva-ātmā (the individual self), distinct from paramātmā (the Supersoul) and brahman (the impersonal effulgence). The verse says one cannot see even ātman — the jīva loses sight of itself under māyā's storm. Doctrinal note: this is not the Advaitin's loss-of-self; it is the storm's covering of self-knowledge.
    • param ca api — or another
      Para — other. Same root as parama (highest) and parātparam (higher than the highest). The verse uses para in its plain 'other person' sense, but the doctrinal echo is present: in the storm one cannot see the other; in māyā more broadly, one cannot see the supreme Para — Bhagavān.
    • vimohitaḥ — being illusioned
      Vi-mohita — thoroughly deluded. Moha is the Gītā's word for the foundational illusion (5.15: ajñānena āvṛtaṁ jñānam tena muhyanti jantavaḥ). The vraja-vāsīs' temporary vimoha is the universal condition in miniature — and the Lord's release of them prefigures every soul's release from māyā.
    • upadrutaḥ — disturbed
      From upa-dru, to run upon, to be assailed. Upadrava is calamity. The Bhāgavatam regularly uses upadrava for the threefold misery (ādhyātmika, ādhibhautika, ādhidaivika). Tṛṇāvarta is the ādhibhautika upadrava here — a fellow-being weaponized by Kaṁsa into a calamity.
    Because of the bits of sand thrown about by Tṛṇāvarta, people could not see themselves or anyone else, and thus they were illusioned and disturbed.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Because of the bits of sand thrown about by Tṛṇāvarta, people could not see themselves or anyone else, and thus they were illusioned and disturbed.
    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    The residents could not see themselves or others (param). The word ca indicates that they could also not hear each other. They could not search (vimohitāḥ).

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Because of the disturbance caused by the demon, no one there knew anything. They could see themselves or others. And they could not even hear anything (ca). Or they could not see themselves or the Lord (param). They could not search at all (vimohitaḥ) because of sand thrown up by Tṛnāvarta. This happened because Kṛṣṇa wanted to go up in the sky.

    Related values & verses
    Related verses
    SB 10.7.20 SB 10.7.21 SB 10.7.22 SB 10.7.24 SB 10.7.25 SB 10.7.26
    Concepts
    Jiva
    Personal Guidance
    When māyā's dust storm blinds us to self and others, we lose all bearings — only Kṛṣṇa, the eye of the storm, remains the unshaken refuge.
    Practical Applications
    • Notice today the moments when sensory overload (notifications, noise, news) blinds you to the souls right in front of you, and pause to chant Kṛṣṇa's name once before responding.
    • Refuse to make any consequential decision while the dust of agitation is still in the air — wait until you can see yourself and others clearly again.
    • When you feel disoriented and cannot see your own next step, stop trying to navigate by your own sight and call on Kṛṣṇa, who alone sees through the storm.
    Analogy

    When the lamp goes out in a crowded room, neighbours suddenly cannot tell themselves from one another — every elbow becomes a stranger's. Tṛṇāvarta's sand does the same to Vraja: in the blindness, even ātmā cannot find ātmā. Only the Lord, who is the witness behind every lamp, sees clearly through the storm.

    Stories
    Arjuna's collapse at the start of the Gītā

    Before Kṛṣṇa spoke, Arjuna could not see himself, could not see his kinsmen, could not see his dharma. Bhāgavatam-style, Mahābhārata-style — the same condition: na śaknomi avasthātum, my limbs fail, my mind reels (Gītā 1.30).

    Kṛṣṇa's response was not to clear the battlefield but to clear Arjuna's sight. The storm of Tṛṇāvarta and the storm of Arjuna's despair are the same situation in different keys: when one cannot see oneself or another, the Lord alone restores vision.

    📝 My note

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.7.24

    ↗
    ఇతి ఖరపవనచక్రపాంశువర్షే సుతపదవీమబలావిలక్ష్య మాతా । అతికరుణమనుస్మరన్త్యశోచద్ భువి పతితా మృతవత్సకా యథా గౌ: ॥ ౨౪ ॥

    Important Words
    • abalā — the innocent woman (literally, the powerless one)
      A-balā — without strength. Standardly applied to women in classical Sanskrit. The verse uses it of Yaśodā at her most powerless moment — exactly when her power as Kṛṣṇa's mother is greatest. Gauḍīya commentaries: it is her abala-ness that pulls Kṛṣṇa back. The Lord cannot bear His devotee's helplessness.
    • ati-karuṇam — very pitifully
      Ati (very) + karuṇa (compassion-evoking). Karuṇa-rasa is one of the classical rasas; in Vaiṣṇava theology it appears as vipralambha — the bhāva of separation. Yaśodā's karuṇa-cry is vipralambha-vātsalya, an extremely high spiritual condition, though it looks only like grief from outside.
    • mṛta-vatsakā — who has lost her calf
      Mṛta (dead) + vatsaka (calf). The simile is from Vraja's own substrate — Yaśodā's grief is described in the language of the cowshed, not the philosophy hall. The Gauḍīya frame: rasa always uses substrate-imagery; vātsalya in Vraja is structurally a cow-and-calf rasa, and the verse's own simile confirms it.
    • patitā — fallen
      From pat — to fall. The same verb later used for Kṛṣṇa's prayer 'tava pāda-paṅkaja-saṁsthitaṁ patati' (CC). Yaśodā's bodily fall mirrors the surrendered devotee's prostration — vātsalya-grief and dāsya-praṇāma share a posture.
    Because of the dust storm stirred up by the strong whirlwind, mother Yaśodā could find no trace of her son, nor could she understand why. Thus she fell down on the ground like a cow who has lost her calf and began to lament very pitifully.
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Because of the dust storm stirred up by the strong whirlwind, mother Yaśodā could find no trace of her son, nor could she understand why. Thus she fell down on the ground like a cow who has lost her calf and began to lament very pitifully.
    Commentary

    Because dust showered down from the whirlwind she could not see the child (avilaksya).

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    By his powers, Kṛṣṇa made himself invisible to all. Though she was enlightened by the Lord through his līlā-śakti and her motherly affection, overcome with pain on not seeing her son, and unable to search for him at all, she began to lament. Because of the dust in the strong whirlwind and harsh wind, she could not understand the path of her son, when he was taken by the demon. She was completely incapable (abalā) of searching. She just fell on the ground. She lamented pitifully, such that even stones and wood would break. Or first she lamented since that was natural, and then she fell on the ground, since she felt great pain from not being able to find him, because of her great affection. An example is given. A cow, not seeing her calf, falls into great disturbance and worry.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Vinaya (Humility / Modesty)Adambhitva (Non-hypocrisy / Authenticity)
    Related verses
    SB 4.29.1 SB 10.7.21 SB 10.7.22 SB 10.7.23 SB 10.7.25 SB 10.7.26 SB 10.7.27 BG 6.35 BG 10.1 BG 12.1 BG 12.5 BG 7.1 BG 2.62
    Personal Guidance
    When Kṛṣṇa vanishes behind māyā's dust storm, even mother Yaśodā collapses — our bewilderment in His apparent absence is the call to deeper surrender.
    Practical Applications
    • Notice today the moments when Kṛṣṇa seems 'lost' to you — in distraction, anxiety, dust-storms of the mind — and call out to Him by name in that exact moment.
    • Refuse to interpret obscurity as absence; when sādhana feels dry, sit and chant anyway, trusting He has not gone anywhere.
    • Speak to someone grieving a loss today not with material consolation but by quietly steadying yourself in the conviction that the soul's relationship with Kṛṣṇa is never severed.
    Analogy

    A cow whose calf has slipped through the fence does not reason about the field's geography; she falls to her knees at the gap and bellows for the calf. Yaśodā's collapse is exactly this cow's collapse — the verse names it directly. Vātsalya-rasa, when its object disappears, abandons every philosophical consolation and becomes a sound.

    Stories
    Kausalyā's lament for Rāma

    When Rāma left for the forest, Kausalyā did not console herself with arguments about karma or the inevitability of separation; she wept and fell upon her bed as if struck (Rāmāyaṇa, Ayodhyā-kāṇḍa). Vālmīki preserves the sound of her grief precisely because it is the sound of pure vātsalya stripped of comfort.

    The Bhāgavatam's portrait of Yaśodā fallen on the ground belongs to the same lineage. Both mothers' grief is itself a teaching: vātsalya-prema, once awakened toward the Lord, cannot be philosophized away.

    Source: vedabase.io

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.7.25

    ↗
    రుదితమనునిశమ్య తత్ర గోప్యో భృశమనుతప్తధియోఽశ్రుపూర్ణముఖ్య: । రురుదురనుపలభ్య నన్దసూనుం పవన ఉపారతపాంశువర్షవేగే ॥ ౨౫ ॥

    Important Words
    • gopyaḥ — the gopīs
      Plural of gopī — Vraja's cowherd-women. In Gauḍīya theology, the gopīs embody the highest reach of bhakti — mādhurya-rasa, the topmost of the five primary rasas. Even in this vātsalya-flavored episode, the gopīs' response prefigures the prema-vilāpa of the later Vraja-līlā.
    • anutapta — lamenting sympathetically
      Anu (after, along with) + tapta (heated, pained). The gopīs do not lament their own loss — they lament after Yaśodā, joining her grief. Caitanya's principle of sādhu-saṅga as shared lamentation for Kṛṣṇa traces directly to this anutapta-dhī of the gopīs.
    • aśru-pūrṇa-mukhyaḥ — their faces full of tears
      Aśru (tears) + pūrṇa (full) + mukha (face). Tears for Kṛṣṇa, in Rūpa Gosvāmī's analysis (Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu 1.3.18), are one of the eight sāttvika-bhāvas — involuntary bodily signs of awakened bhāva. The gopīs' tears are not symptoms of grief; they are ornaments of prema.
    • nanda-sūnum — the son of Nanda Mahārāja
      Nanda (the father) + sūnu (son). The verse names Kṛṣṇa by His Vraja-relationship, not by His cosmic title. This is the Gauḍīya signature: in Vraja, Kṛṣṇa is approached through His mādhurya-relations (son of Nanda, beloved of Rādhā), not through His aiśvarya-titles.
    When the force of the dust storm and the winds subsided, Yaśodā’s friends, the other gopīs, approached mother Yaśodā, hearing her pitiful crying. Not seeing Kṛṣṇa present, they too felt very much aggrieved and joined mother Yaśodā in crying, their eyes full of tears.
    Purport

    This attachment of the gopīs to Kṛṣṇa is wonderful and transcendental. The center of all the activities of the gopīs was Kṛṣṇa. When Kṛṣṇa was there they were happy, and when Kṛṣṇa was not there, they were unhappy. Thus when mother Yaśodā was lamenting Kṛṣṇa’s absence, the other ladies also began to cry.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    When the force of the dust storm and the winds subsided, Yaśodā's friends, the other gopīs, approached mother Yaśodā, hearing her pitiful crying. Not seeing Kṛṣṇa present, they too felt very much aggrieved and joined mother Yaśodā in crying, their eyes full of tears.
    Commentary

    When the dust cleared from the blowing wind, the gopis heard crying from Yasoda’s house, they all came from far off and also began crying.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    When the wind filled with dust died down, the neighboring gopīs, hearing repeatedly crying within Yaśodā’s house (tatra), and going to the house and not seeing Kṛṣṇa, became filled with sorrow and, shedding tears, wept. Instead of anutapta-dhiyaḥ sometimes anurakta-dhiyaḥ (they were attached to Nanda and Yaśodā) is seen. The son of Nanda is mentioned. This indicates that following Nanda’s lamentation everyone lamented.

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    In that place which was continually full of bliss (tatra), they heard Yaśodā crying, since the loud noise of the wind had stopped. They became greatly aggrieved and their eyes filled with tears. They went to the place (tatra) where Yaśodā was crying, when the wind ceased to shower dust. Not finding the son of Nanda who gave joy to Vraja at the side of Yaśodā, all the gopīs cried. Instead of anutapta-dhiyaḥ sometimes anurakta-dhiyaḥ (they were attached to Nanda and Yaśodā) is seen. Nanda and all the people of Vraja cried.

    Related values & verses
    Related values
    Vinaya (Humility / Modesty)Adambhitva (Non-hypocrisy / Authenticity)

    SB 4.29.1 SB 10.7.22 SB 10.7.23 SB 10.7.24 SB 10.7.26 SB 10.7.27 SB 10.7.28 BG 10.1 BG 6.35 BG 12.1 BG 12.5 BG 2.62 BG 7.1
    Related lectures
    • Revati Raman Pr SB 10-07-25 Telugu Translation - 2013-09-24 ISKCON Tirupati
      His Grace Revati Raman Prabhu· 2013-09-24· Telugu
    Personal Guidance
    When Kṛṣṇa is the center, His absence aches and His presence delights — let your daily emotional weather reveal where your heart truly rests.
    Practical Applications
    • Notice this evening which absence in your day caused real grief — and ask honestly whether Kṛṣṇa's absence registers with even a fraction of that weight.
    • Speak to one fellow devotee today about Kṛṣṇa specifically, so that your saṅga becomes, like the gopīs', a circle whose center is Him.
    • Refuse to let a day pass in which you do not consciously feel His presence in chanting or His absence in distraction — train the heart to track Him.
    Analogy

    Around a single flame, the lamps drawn near catch fire one after another, and when the central flame seems to gutter, every lit lamp flickers. The gopīs are the lamps; Kṛṣṇa is the flame. His absence ripples through their bodies as tears, because every joy in their lives has been lit from Him.

    Stories
    Caitanya at Jagannātha Purī weeping for Kṛṣṇa

    In Gambhīrā, Caitanya Mahāprabhu in His latter years entered the gopīs' mood of separation so completely that He would weep, cry out Kṛṣṇa's names, and lacerate His face on the walls in His sleep (CC Antya 14). The Six Gosvāmīs preserved this as the highest reach of vipralambha-mādhurya.

    The gopīs in 10.7.25 are the original source-material for what Caitanya later embodied: when Kṛṣṇa cannot be found, true love does not console itself; it weeps with His devotees and refuses to be separated alone.

    Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam 10.7.26

    ↗
    తృణావర్త: శాన్తరయో వాత్యారూపధరో హరన్ । కృష్ణం నభోగతో గన్తుం నాశక్నోద్ భూరిభారభృత్ ॥ ౨౬ ॥
    tṛṇāvartaḥ śānta-rayo
    [trinaavartah shaanta-rayo]
    vātyā-rūpa-dharo haran
    [vaatyaa-roopa-dharo haran]
    kṛṣṇaṁ nabho-gato gantuṁ
    [krishnam nabho-gato gantum]
    nāśaknod bhūri-bhāra-bhṛt
    [naashaknod bhoori-bhaara-bhrit]
    Word-for-word
    tṛṇāvartaḥ — the demon Tṛṇāvarta śānta - rayaḥ — the force of the blast reduced vātyā - rūpa - dharaḥ — who had assumed the form of a forceful whirlwind haran — and had thus taken away kṛṣṇam — Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead nabhaḥ - gataḥ — went up to the top of the sky gantum — to go further na aśaknot — was not able bhūri - bhāra - bhṛt — because Kṛṣṇa then became more powerful and heavy than the demon .
    Blended reading (Sanskrit + English inline)
    tṛṇāvartaḥ śānta-rayo
    tṛṇāvartaḥ
    the demon Tṛṇāvarta
    śānta - rayaḥ
    the force of the blast reduced
    vātyā-rūpa-dharo haran
    vātyā - rūpa - dharaḥ
    who had assumed the form of a forceful whirlwind
    haran

    kṛṣṇaṁ nabho-gato gantuṁ
    kṛṣṇam

    nabhaḥ - gataḥ
    went up to the top of the sky
    gantum
    to go further
    nāśaknod bhūri-bhāra-bhṛt
    na aśaknot
    was not able
    bhūri - bhāra - bhṛt
    because Kṛṣṇa then became more powerful and heavy than the demon .
    Important Words
    • śānta-rayaḥ — the force of the blast reduced
      Śānta (calmed) + raya (speed, force). The same śānta which names śānta-rasa — the peace of self-realization. Here the demon's speed itself is śānta-ed; even Tṛṇāvarta's whirlwind is brought to a yogic-style stillness by Kṛṣṇa's weight. The Lord pacifies even the storm-form.
    • vātyā-rūpa-dharaḥ — who had assumed the form of a forceful whirlwind
      Vātyā (storm) + rūpa (form) + dhara (holder). The demon holds a form — he is not the form. The Gauḍīya distinction: jīvas wear rūpas; Bhagavān is His rūpa (sac-cid-ānanda-vigraha). Tṛṇāvarta's rūpa is a costume; Kṛṣṇa's rūpa is His being.
    • nabhaḥ-gataḥ — went up to the top of the sky
      Nabhas (sky) + gata (gone). Nabhas is the Vedic word for ākāśa. The demon's apparent ascent into the sky is the false ascent of asuric power — Hiraṇyakaśipu boasting of reaching the heavens. The Bhāgavatam's pattern: asuric ascent ends where divine weight begins.
    • bhūri-bhāra-bhṛt — becoming heavier than what could be borne
      Bhūri (much) + bhāra (weight) + bhṛt (bearer). The compound describes Kṛṣṇa as the bearer of immense weight — but the demon is now under it. The verb-noun bhṛt is Vedic: He who bears the universe (jagad-bhṛt) is here resting on the throat of a single demon, which cannot bear what bears all.
    Having assumed the form of a forceful whirlwind, the demon Tṛṇāvarta took Kṛṣṇa very high in the sky, but when Kṛṣṇa became heavier than the demon, the demon had to stop his force and could go no further.
    Purport

    Here is a competition in yogic power between Kṛṣṇa and Tṛṇāvartāsura. By practicing mystic yoga, asuras generally attain some perfection in the eight siddhis, or perfections, namely aṇimā, laghimā, mahimā, prāpti, prākāmya, īśitva, vaśitva and kāmāvasāyitā. But although a demon may acquire such powers to a very limited extent, he cannot compete with the mystic power of Kṛṣṇa, for Kṛṣṇa is Yogeśvara, the source of all mystic power ( yatra yogeśvaro hariḥ ). No one can compete with Kṛṣṇa. Sometimes, of course, having acquired a fragmental portion of Kṛṣṇa’s mystic power, asuras demonstrate their power to the foolish public and assert themselves to be God, not knowing that God is the supreme Yogeśvara. Here also we see that Tṛṇāvarta assumed the mahimā-siddhi and took Kṛṣṇa away as if Kṛṣṇa were an ordinary child. But Kṛṣṇa also became a mystic mahimā-siddha. When mother Yaśodā was carrying Him, He became so heavy that His mother, who was usually accustomed to carrying Him, could not bear Him and had to place Him down on the ground. Thus Tṛṇāvarta had been able to take Kṛṣṇa away in the presence of mother Yaśodā. But when Kṛṣṇa, high in the sky, assumed the mahimā-siddhi, the demon, unable to go further, was obliged to stop his force and come down according to Kṛṣṇa’s desire. One should not, therefore, compete with Kṛṣṇa’s mystic power. Devotees automatically have all mystic power, but they do not like to compete with Kṛṣṇa. Instead, they fully surrender to Kṛṣṇa, and their yogic power is demonstrated by Kṛṣṇa’s mercy. Devotees can show mystic yoga so powerful that a demon could not even dream of it, but they never try to demonstrate it for their personal sense gratification. Whatever they do is for the service of the Lord, and therefore they are always in a position superior to that of the demons. There are many karmīs, yogīs and jñānīs who artificially try to compete with Kṛṣṇa, and thus ordinary, foolish people who do not care to hear Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam from authorities consider some rascal yogī to be Bhagavān, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. At the present moment there are many so-called bābās who present themselves as incarnations of God by showing some insignificant mystic wonder, and foolish people regard them as God because of lacking knowledge of Kṛṣṇa.

    — A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda
    Gauḍīya Ācāryas
    Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura Gauḍīya
    Translation
    Having assumed the form of a forceful whirlwind, the demon Tṛṇāvarta took Kṛṣṇa very high in the sky, but when Kṛṣṇa became heavier than the demon, the demon had to stop his force and could go no further.
    Commentary

    At first Krsna was as light as a normal child and Trnavarta lifted him up high in the sky. But then Krsna became heavy, and Trnavarta slowed down. He was unable to carry him further and stopped moving.

    Jīva Gosvāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Because the people were crying in pain, Kṛṣṇa decided to end his pastime of travelling in the air and decided to kill the demon. In the shape of a storm, the demon took Kṛṣṇa into the air and there, because Kṛṣṇa manifested weight, the demon felt overburdened (bhūri-bhāra-bhṛt). He was not able to go further (śānta-rayaḥ).

    Śrīdhara Svāmī Gauḍīya
    Commentary

    Because the people were crying in pain, Kṛṣṇa decided to end his pastime of travelling in the air and decided to kill the demon. The demon carried him in order to take him to a great distance (bhūri), but Kṛṣṇa manifested heaviness by his nature or to show his power. Or he carried Kṛṣna who was very (bhūri) heavy.

    Related values & verses
    Related values

    Related verses

      His Grace Madhumangal Prabhu· 2013-11-26· Hindi
